### Task 8: Corpus, `exhaustive: false`, spec history, name-lock proof and fuzz (gates)

**Files:** none in the repository; the scripts below live in a scratch directory (`$S`, outside the repository).

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the generator of task 7 (`generate`, `diff`, `--fresh-names`), the M11 tool (`corpus check`, `fuzz`).
- Produces: the numbers of "Corpus run" below; no commit.

This task changes no file. It runs the brief's gates on the whole of M12 and records what they show; a gate that fails sends the fix back to the task that owns the code (the history check found one: the wording of task 4).

- [ ] **Step 1: The corpus is unchanged**

```bash
cd packages/typed_openapi_tool && dart run bin/corpus.dart check
```

Expected: `40 clients, 38 generated, 27759 files; 0 differences from the lock.` and exit `0` (about 5 minutes with a warm cache). `corpus.lock` is not changed. Each client is generated twice by the corpus run; the second run reads the `typed_openapi.lock` the first one wrote, names with it, and writes nothing, so the lock reproduces the fresh names on 38 real specs.

- [ ] **Step 2: Every corpus client analyzes clean with `exhaustive: false`**

In the corpus package of the `raw` mode (`packages/typed_openapi_tool/.dart_tool/corpus/out/raw`), copy `typed_openapi.yaml` to `typed_openapi.exh.yaml`, add `defaults:\n  exhaustive: false` at its top, and run:

```bash
dart run ../../../../../typed_openapi/bin/typed_openapi.dart generate -c typed_openapi.exh.yaml
grep -rl 'sealed class' lib | wc -l                 # 0
grep -rh 'abstract interface class' lib | wc -l    # 4940
dart analyze --fatal-infos lib                     # No issues found!
rm typed_openapi.exh.yaml
```

Expected: every generated client is written (artemis is skipped, as the corpus expects); each line counts only `switch-breaking` changes (Stripe 1745, GitHub 796), since sealed types became interfaces; no `sealed class` is left and 4940 bases are `abstract interface`; the analyzer reports no issue. Run `corpus check` again afterwards to restore the package.

- [ ] **Step 3: `diff` on real spec history**

Compile the CLI once (`dart compile exe packages/typed_openapi/bin/typed_openapi.dart -o $S/bin/toa`) and download two pinned versions of four corpus specs (the only network use of M12) into `$S/history/<name>/spec/old.<ext>` and `new.<ext>`:

| Spec | Old | New |
| --- | --- | --- |
| immich | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/immich-app/immich/v3.2.4/open-api/immich-openapi-specs.json` | `.../v3.3.3/...` |
| kubernetes | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/v1.36.0/api/openapi-spec/v3/api__v1_openapi.json` | `.../v1.37.0/...` |
| openai | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/openai-openapi/e95c0fe615f45a19e8878af923a729948445a6bd/openapi.yaml` | `.../c396f25f2f8112d7761097b7b0217808dc65ddd4/openapi.yaml` |
| stripe | `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/stripe/openapi/v2400/openapi/spec3.json` | `.../v2586/...` |

**Scratch** `$S/bin/history.sh`:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Usage: history.sh <dir> <ext>
# <dir>/spec/old.<ext> and <dir>/spec/new.<ext> are two versions of one spec.
# Generates the old one (writing typed_openapi.lock), then diffs the new one
# against the lock, then generates the new one with --fresh-names from a copy
# of the old lock to count the renames the lock prevents.
set -u
S=$(dirname "$0")
TOA="$S/toa"
dir=$1
ext=$2
name=$(basename "$dir")
work="$dir/work"
fresh="$dir/fresh"
rm -rf "$work" "$fresh"
mkdir -p "$work" "$fresh"
for w in "$work" "$fresh"; do
  printf 'clients:\n  %s: {input: cur.%s, output: gen}\n' "$name" "$ext" > "$w/typed_openapi.yaml"
  cp "$dir/spec/old.$ext" "$w/cur.$ext"
done
(cd "$work" && "$TOA" generate > ../old_generate.txt 2>&1; echo "old generate exit $?")
cp "$work/typed_openapi.lock" "$fresh/typed_openapi.lock"
cp -r "$work/gen" "$fresh/gen"
cp "$dir/spec/new.$ext" "$work/cur.$ext"
cp "$dir/spec/new.$ext" "$fresh/cur.$ext"
(cd "$work" && "$TOA" diff --format json > ../diff.json 2> ../diff_err.txt; echo "diff exit $?")
(cd "$work" && "$TOA" diff > ../diff.txt 2>/dev/null)
(cd "$work" && "$TOA" generate > ../new_generate.txt 2>&1; echo "new generate exit $?")
(cd "$fresh" && "$TOA" generate --fresh-names > ../fresh_generate.txt 2>&1; echo "fresh generate exit $?")
echo "locked: $(grep '^API of' "$dir/new_generate.txt")"
echo "fresh:  $(grep '^API of' "$dir/fresh_generate.txt")"
echo "renames in diff: $(grep -c 'was renamed to' "$dir/diff.txt")"
echo "TOA570 kept: $(grep -c 'TOA570' "$dir/new_generate.txt")"
```

Run `$S/bin/history.sh $S/history/<name> json` (`yaml` for openai) for each spec. Expected (the `locked` and `fresh` lines are equal: no name of these histories collides):

| Spec | breaking | switch-breaking | wire | additive | docs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| immich v3.2.4 -> v3.3.3 | 9 | 9 | 0 | 25 | 1 |
| kubernetes v1.36.0 -> v1.37.0 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 28 | 14 |
| openai e95c0fe -> c396f25 | 13 | 9 | 6 | 68 | 26 |
| stripe v2400 -> v2586 | 142 | 42 | 2 | 576 | 176 |

Read the reports against the specs: Stripe's 142 breaking are 112 changed types (a field whose `anyOf` gained a branch becomes a union), 25 removed members or types, 4 new required members and 1 union that became a model; its 42 switch-breaking are new enum values. Every line must be explainable by the spec change; a line that reads wrong is a defect of the task that owns the wording or the rule.

- [ ] **Step 4: The name lock on real specs**

**Scratch** `$S/bin/probe.sh`:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Usage: probe.sh <dir> <client>
# <dir>/work holds a generated JSON spec cur.json and its typed_openapi.lock.
# Adds to the spec a component schema named like an inline type of the lock
# and, first in `paths`, an operation whose operationId gives the name of an
# existing method in the same tag, then diffs with the lock (locked names)
# and generates with --fresh-names to show what the lock prevents.
set -u
S=$(dirname "$0")
TOA="$S/toa"
dir=$1
client=$2
lock="$dir/work/typed_openapi.lock"
inline=$(jq -r --arg c "$client" '.clients[$c] | to_entries[] | select(.key | startswith("#")) | select(.key | test("^#/components/schemas/[^/]+$") | not) | select(.value.kind=="model" or .value.kind=="enumeration") | .value.name' "$lock" | head -1)
read -r method verb path < <(jq -r --arg c "$client" '.clients[$c] | to_entries[] | select(.value.kind=="group") | .value.members | to_entries[] | select(.value.kind=="method") | .value.name + " " + .value.facts.method + " " + .value.facts.path' "$lock" | head -1)
echo "inline type: ${inline:-none}; method: $method ($verb $path)"
for mode in locked fresh; do
  w="$dir/probe_$mode"
  rm -rf "$w"
  cp -r "$dir/work" "$w"
  jq --arg t "$inline" --arg m "$method" --arg v "$(echo "$verb" | tr 'A-Z' 'a-z')" --arg p "$path" '
    (.paths[$p][$v].tags // []) as $tags
    | (if $t == "" then {} else {"$ref": ("#/components/schemas/" + $t)} end) as $schema
    | (if $t == "" then . else .components.schemas[$t] = {type: "object", properties: {probe: {type: "string"}}} end)
    | ($m | gsub("(?<u>[A-Z])"; "_" + (.u | ascii_downcase))) as $id
    | .paths = ({"/0-m12-probe": {get: {operationId: $id, tags: $tags, responses: {"200": {description: "ok", content: {"application/json": {schema: $schema}}}}}}} + .paths)
  ' "$dir/work/cur.json" > "$w/cur.json"
done
(cd "$dir/probe_locked" && "$TOA" diff > ../probe_diff.txt 2>/dev/null; echo "diff exit $?")
(cd "$dir/probe_locked" && "$TOA" generate > ../probe_locked.txt 2>&1)
(cd "$dir/probe_fresh" && "$TOA" generate --fresh-names > ../probe_fresh.txt 2>&1)
echo "locked: $(grep '^API of' "$dir/probe_locked.txt")"
echo "fresh:  $(grep '^API of' "$dir/probe_fresh.txt")"
echo "locked diff:"; sed 's/^/  /' "$dir/probe_diff.txt" | head -12
```

The new operation's `operationId` is the snake case of the method's name (`create_api_key` for `createApiKey`), so it is a different id (an equal one is TOA504) that gives the same Dart name. Run it on the `work` directories of step 3 for immich, kubernetes and stripe. Expected:

| Spec | Added | With the lock | `--fresh-names` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| immich | component `CalendarHeatmapResponseDtoSeriesItem` (an inline model's name), `GET /0-m12-probe` named `createApiKey` | 0 breaking, 2 additive: `Model CalendarHeatmapResponseDtoSeriesItem2 was added.`, `Method APIKeysApi.createApiKey2 was added.` | 3 breaking, 2 additive |
| kubernetes | (no inline model or enum in the lock) an operation named `connectCoreV1DeleteNamespacedPodProxy` | 0 breaking, 1 additive: `Method CoreV1Api.connectCoreV1DeleteNamespacedPodProxy2 was added.` | 4 breaking, 1 additive |
| stripe | component `AccountBusinessType` (the inline enum of `account.business_type`), an operation named `deleteAccountsAccount` | 0 breaking, 2 additive: `Model AccountBusinessType2 was added.`, `Method DefaultApi.deleteAccountsAccount2 was added.` | 8 breaking, 2 additive |

With the lock every old name stays and the new schema and operation take the suffix; without it the old ones are renamed, which is breaking.

### Task 9: Architecture, the API lock and the manifest, roadmap verdicts (docs)

**Files:**
- Modify: `CLAUDE.md`
- Modify: `docs/roadmap.md`
- Modify: `packages/typed_openapi/doc/cli.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything tasks 1-7 added, the numbers of task 8.
- Produces: nothing a later task uses.

`CLAUDE.md` describes the new stages (`snapshot/`, `diff/`), the config readers of task 1, `exhaustive`, the name lock and its codes, the new `generate` flags and the three files a project now has (the output's manifest `.typed_openapi.json`, `typed_openapi.lock` next to the config, a baseline); `fixedCodes` grows to TOA830-TOA835. `doc/cli.md` says that the lock is not the manifest and that the manifest's inputs hash does not cover the lock (a lock change alone never rewrites a client). `docs/roadmap.md` links this plan from the M12 row, gives a verdict on every row parked for M12, and adds the rows moved from M12 ("Deferred" below).

**Diff** (apply from the repository root with `git apply`):

````diff
diff --git a/packages/typed_openapi/doc/cli.md b/packages/typed_openapi/doc/cli.md
index e87835b..6381207 100644
--- a/packages/typed_openapi/doc/cli.md
+++ b/packages/typed_openapi/doc/cli.md
@@ -123,7 +123,7 @@ The second run writes nothing, because the output is the same. Ownership, locks
 | `additive` | A new type, method, or optional field or argument. |
 | `docs` | Only doc comments or `@Deprecated` changed. |
 
-A run of some clients keeps the sections of the others, and drops the section of a client the config no longer declares. `generate --locked` changes no lock: a client whose API differs from its section, or has none, is skipped with TOA834, and so is a section of a client the config no longer declares. A lock that cannot be read, such as one with merge-conflict markers, is TOA835 and the run writes no client: resolve the conflict, or delete the file to start from the current API. This file is not the lock of an output directory (`.<output>.typed_openapi.lock`), which only keeps two runs apart.
+A run of some clients keeps the sections of the others, and drops the section of a client the config no longer declares. `generate --locked` changes no lock: a client whose API differs from its section, or has none, is skipped with TOA834, and so is a section of a client the config no longer declares. A lock that cannot be read, such as one with merge-conflict markers, is TOA835 and the run writes no client: resolve the conflict, or delete the file to start from the current API. This file is not the lock of an output directory (`.<output>.typed_openapi.lock`), which only keeps two runs apart. Nor is it the manifest `.typed_openapi.json` inside an output, which lists the files `generate` wrote so that it replaces only those: the manifest is rewritten with the output and goes when the output is deleted, while `typed_openapi.lock` records what the code declares and stays. The inputs hash of the manifest covers the spec and the config, not `typed_openapi.lock`.
 
 The lock also keeps names. Without it, a schema added to the spec can take the name of a type that existed: a new component `PetStatus` is named before the inline enum `Pet.status`, which was `PetStatus` and would become `PetStatus2`. With the lock, every command that reads the spec (`check`, `generate`, `diff`, `baseline`) names each model, enum, union and method as the lock has it, and the new one gets the suffix (TOA551). A name the lock keeps that a run without it would not give is reported as TOA570 (info); an override name or a custom type that takes a kept name wins, and the type it belonged to is named anew (TOA569, warning). `generate --fresh-names` names everything from the spec alone and writes those names to the lock; the line of each client counts the renames as `breaking`. Fields, enum values, arguments, group classes and the helper types (status types, event unions, parts classes, variant classes) are not kept yet.
````

## Corpus run

`dart run bin/corpus.dart check` on the final tree (task 8, step 1; cache and work directories in the tool's `.dart_tool/corpus`, about 5.5 minutes): `40 clients, 38 generated, 27759 files; 0 differences from the lock.` `corpus.lock` is byte-identical before and after M12: the refactor of task 1, `exhaustive` (default `true`), the snapshot, the lock file and the name lock change no generated file of the 38 clients (artemis is skipped for TOA009, as expected). Every corpus client is generated twice by the corpus run; the second run names with the `typed_openapi.lock` the first one wrote and writes nothing.

`exhaustive: false` on the whole corpus (task 8, step 2): 0 `sealed class` left, 4940 `abstract interface class` bases, `dart analyze --fatal-infos lib` clean; the API line of each client counts only `switch-breaking` changes (Stripe 1745, GitHub 796).

## Fuzz

`dart run bin/fuzz.dart --seeds 200` on the final tree: `200 seeds: 1200 builds, 10907 files, 17 sampled; 0 problems.` M12 changes no emitted code with the default config, so the fuzz is the M11 fuzz and passes unchanged.

## Spec history

`typed_openapi diff` between two published versions of four corpus specs (task 8, step 3; the downloads are the only network use of M12):

| Spec | Versions | breaking | switch-breaking | wire | additive | docs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| immich | v3.2.4 -> v3.3.3 | 9 | 9 | 0 | 25 | 1 |
| kubernetes | v1.36.0 -> v1.37.0 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 28 | 14 |
| openai | `e95c0fe` -> `c396f25` | 13 | 9 | 6 | 68 | 26 |
| stripe | v2400 -> v2586 | 142 | 42 | 2 | 576 | 176 |

Stripe's 142 breaking changes are 112 changed types (mostly a field whose `anyOf` gained a branch and so became a union), 25 removed members or types, 4 new required members and 1 union that became a model; its 42 switch-breaking changes are new enum values. Every line read was explained by the spec change. The generate line with the lock equals the line with `--fresh-names` for all four, and no TOA570 is reported: no name of these histories collided, so the lock kept nothing a fresh run would have renamed.

## Name lock proof

A spec version that would rename (task 8, step 4: a component named like an inline type of the lock, and an operation whose `operationId` gives the name of an existing method of its group):

| Spec | With the lock (`diff`, exit 0) | `--fresh-names` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| immich | 0 breaking, 2 additive (`CalendarHeatmapResponseDtoSeriesItem2`, `APIKeysApi.createApiKey2`) | 3 breaking, 2 additive |
| kubernetes | 0 breaking, 1 additive (`CoreV1Api.connectCoreV1DeleteNamespacedPodProxy2`; its lock has no inline model or enum) | 4 breaking, 1 additive |
| stripe | 0 breaking, 2 additive (`AccountBusinessType2`, `DefaultApi.deleteAccountsAccount2`) | 8 breaking, 2 additive |

| Defect | Found by | In this plan |

| The lock path was resolved against the process's working directory, not the config's, so `generate -c sub/typed_openapi.yaml` from another directory read and wrote the wrong lock | the CLI tests of task 7 | Fixed: task 7 (`ApiLockNames` takes `DisplayPaths.absolute(config.file)`) |
| `spec_checker.dart` passed 300 lines once it read the lock | the size ratchet | Fixed: task 7 (`ApiLockNames` and the run key moved out) |
| An operation whose `operationId` repeats another (TOA504) is an error, so the client is not generated and `diff` reports its methods as removed and added; the first probe hit it | the name-lock probe | Not a defect: the probe now writes the snake case of the method's name, a different id with the same Dart name |

| M11: the generated code needs `JsonReader.constant` to return a `bool`, with no gate tying the client version to the generator ("M12 (API snapshot) or M13") | Re-parked for M13: the snapshot is the API of a generated client; the client packages' own API is out of the M12 spec's scope |

## Deferred

Written into the roadmap's "Moved from M12" (task 9):

- The lock keeps the names of models, enums, unions and methods only ("Decisions taken" 6). Fields, enum values, arguments, group classes and the helper types are named from the spec; a collision there is rare (their scopes are per type) and `diff` reports it as `breaking`.
- A schema renamed in the spec is not detected by structure (the spec's "Out of scope"); `diff` reports removed plus added with a `name:` fix.
- `generate --fresh-names` is the only way to drop kept names; the name of a type the spec no longer declares is freed by itself (TOA571).

## Self-review

Spec decisions: 1 - task 3 (`SnapshotBuilder` from the IR: types with kinds, fields, variants, values, methods with parameters, returns and status cases, wire facts; identities by spec location or `operationId`/`METHOD /path`; no analyzer; deterministic, `snapshot_builder_test.dart`). 2 - task 4 (the five categories, "Decisions taken" 3, a test per rule). 3 - task 2 (`exhaustive` in `defaults` and per client, default `true`, `abstract interface` bases, the override wins) and task 4 (a new variant of a non-exhaustive type is `additive`). 4 - task 6 (`diff [clients]`, `text`/`json`/`markdown`, `--fail-on`, `--from`/`--to` for two lock files, writes nothing) and task 5 (the line of `generate`). 5 - task 7 (kept names, new identities take free names, TOA569/TOA571 for a kept name that cannot be kept, `--fresh-names`), proved on three corpus specs (task 8). 6 - task 5 (one `typed_openapi.lock` next to the config with a section per client, written by `generate`, read by `diff`, `--locked` with TOA834). 7 - task 4 (`RenameHints`: removed plus added, breaking, with a ready `name:` override). Out of scope respected: no structural rename detection, no codemods, the client packages untouched.

Types across tasks: `ApiSnapshot`, `SnapshotType`, `SnapshotMember`, `TypeKind`, `MemberKind` (task 3) are used with the same names by tasks 4-7; `ApiChange`, `ChangeCategory` (task 4) by tasks 5 and 6; `ApiLock`, `ApiLockRun`, `ChangeCounts` (task 5) by tasks 6 and 7; `GroupIr.key` (task 3) by task 7's method keys. Each task's block compiles on its own in the replay.

Proportion: the plan carries the code whole because it was replayed from its blocks (the M11 format); the prose of each task is the decisions the code does not show.

## Open questions

Each is a choice the spec leaves open; the plan implements the default given.

1. **A lock that cannot be read fails every command** (TOA835, an error and a fixed code): `check` and `diff` exit `1`, `generate` writes no client. Alternative: `check` warns and names without the lock.
2. **TOA571 (a kept name whose type is gone) is an info.** Alternative: a warning, so that a dropped schema is noticed before the next `generate` frees its name.
3. **The events method of an operation is keyed `<op>#stream`** (and `METHOD /path#stream` in the name lock), so its name is kept apart from the body method's. Alternative: key it by the stream method's own name.
4. **The manifest's inputs hash does not cover `typed_openapi.lock`**: a lock change alone never rewrites a client, and the names the lock gives are covered by the files' own hashes. Alternative: hash the client's lock section too.
5. **Task 7 moves `NameScope` and the stream method's name into their own files in the feature commit**, not in a separate refactor commit, because the move is what lets the allowlisted `names.dart` and the 300-line `event_streams.dart` take the lock without growing; the moved code is unchanged and the corpus output is byte-identical. Alternative: split it into a `refactor(...)` commit before task 7.
6. **Only models, enums, unions and methods are locked** ("Decisions taken" 6; see "Deferred"). Alternative: also lock fields, enum values and arguments, at the cost of a lock that pins the spelling of every member.
7. **`exhaustive` enters the run key only when it is false** ("Decisions taken" 9), so the manifests of existing outputs keep their inputs hash. Alternative: always write it, which makes the next `generate` of every project rewrite its manifest once.
